### q_5ff053 (ranking_v2, ranking, bigram_lm) score=1.00 noKB_score=1.00 gold=C<B<A<D<E pred=C<B<A<D<E cited=K1002,K1019,K1070 retrieved=none
explanation: <cited>K1002,K1019,K1070</cited>
<answer>C<B<A<D<E</answer>
comment: Identical GRU architectures made optimizer progress the main discriminator in this short, low-rate regime. K1002 supported Adagrad .001 over Adam .00003 and Adagrad .0001: its 33× rate advantage over Adam offsets accumulator slowdown. K1070 supplied the low-rate RMSprop-over-Adam preference, correctly ordering B before A. K1019’s bigram-specific momentum-SGD calibration supported placing E last, behind even low-rate Adagrad D. These scoped tendencies predicted the complete order without invoking capacity, architecture, or high-rate saturation effects.
hypothesis: -

### q_9d5a3c (ranking_v3, ranking, synthetic_tabular_classification) score=0.50 noKB_score=0.50 gold=A<E<D<C<B pred=A<E<C<B<D cited=K1009,K1015,K1016,K1033,K1073 retrieved=none
explanation: <cited>K1009,K1015,K1016,K1033,K1073</cited>
<answer>A<E<C<B<D</answer>
comment: I over-penalized D’s deep plain stack: its single late LN beat both two-LN alternatives, despite greater depth. Limited intermediate normalization may preserve useful interaction features, but that mechanism remains unmeasured. K1009’s high-progress shallow preference helped place A/E; K1015 did not justify putting D last, and K1033 warns against transferring depth preferences across budgets. Backtesting “fewer LN wins” at shared adaptive optimizer settings and lr×steps≥0.1 gave 19/28 history hits across four questions, with nine misses. Lab sparse comparisons gave 14/14 hits across six sets, mostly confounding fewer LN with shallower depth. These checks do not establish D>C>B or a defensible complete-ranking rule.
hypothesis: -

SGD: Δ≈lr·T·|g|/(1−m) with raw grad |g|≈0.01 (bigram LM 0.003, MSE regression with momentum 0.03 — K1019), i.e. plain SGD ≈ Adam at lr/100, SGD m=0.9 ≈ Adam at lr/10. The largest-Δ choice wins 85% of optimizer-only and ~85% of mixed questions (after the lr-ceiling exceptions); a 1.5-3× Δ edge usually beats any architecture advantage.
